SB: Hon'ble Shri Satish K. Aenihotri, J.
(Passed on ..S..\-day
of July, 2008)
In this bateh of petitions,the common question oflawis asto whether the
Rojgar Sahayaks, Technical Assistants, Accountants and others, appointed
for a contract period ofone year are entitled to be considered for extension of
the term of appointment in the subsequent year, secondly, whether the.
petitioners are entitled to grant of preference/weightage on account of their
experience oftheirwork for one year on contracf basis.
Following writ petitions i.e. W.P.(S) No. 7482/2007, 7589/2007, 7590/2007,
7595/2007, 7596/2007, 75.98/2007, 7678/2007, 7679/2007, 7680/2007,
7720/2007, 7721/2007, 7724/2007, 7725/2007, 7726/2007, 7729/2007,
7747/2007, 7749/2007, 7750/2007, 7751/2007; 7753/2007, 775.4/2007,
7756/2007, 7757/2007, 7758/2007, 7759/2007, 01/2008, 02/2008, 194/2008,
195/2008, 242/2008, 243/2008, 244/2008, 245/2008, 269/2008, 311/2008,
312/2008, 507/2008, 970/2008, 975/2008, 1077/2008, 1170/2008,
1171/2008, 1187/2008, 1215/2008, 1556/2008 7722/2007, 7727/2007,
1212/2008, 2115/2008 and 2334/2008 deal With appointment of Rojgar
Sahayak, W.P.(S) 7101/2007, 7307/2007, 7325/2007, 7631/2007, 7706/2007,
2216/2008 and 7069/2007 deal with appointment of Technical Assistants,
W.P. (S) No. 7006/2007 and 7312/2007 deal with appointment of
Accountants, W.P.(S) No. 7743/2007 deals with appointment of Technical
Assistants, Accountants and Assistant Grade II.
Rojgar Sahayaks, Accountants and Technical Assistants and others are
appointed to assist the District Programme Coordinator and the PrograniiTie
Officers for the effective implementation of the Employment Guarantee
Scheme. TUe petitioners were accordingly appointed on contract basis in the

t»
4.
99
year 2005, 2006 and 2007 for a period of one year with a condition that the
contract appointment would be for a period ofone year, confidential report t)f
the employees would be maintained to assess their suitability Ibr
consideration in the subsequent contract appointment. The appointments
were made pursuant to the advertisement dated 25.3.2006, in case of
Accountants and Technical Assistants, issued by the State Government,
wherein it was clearly provided that the appointment ofthe petitioners are on
contract basis for a period of one year and their period may be extended
further for one more year. It was further provided in the advertisement that
the contract appointment is made in accordance with Chhattisgarh Civil Sewa
(Saiiivida Niyukti) Niyam, 2004 (for short 'the Rules, 2004'). In case of
Rojgar Sahayaks, there was no uniformity in the appointment orders. Some
of the appointment orders issued by some of the Districts namely Raigarh
and Jashpur provide for recording of confidential reports and assessment of
their work for considering their cases for further extension of the term of
appointment. In other cases, no. such provision was made. Applicability of
Rules, 2004 was also not mentioned in some of the appointment orders. The
advertisements were issued at District level without providing for terms and
conditions ofappointment. In the meantime, a fresh advertisement was issuud
in November, 2007 inviting applications for appointments on the post ol
Rojgar Sahayak, Technical Assistant and Accountants. Thus, these petitions
are filed challenging issue ofthe advertisement and selection thereofignoring
the terms and conditions of initial advertisement and the appointment order
that their cases may be considered for extension ofthe period ofappointment
on the basis oftheir perfonTiance during their services.
In some of the appointment orders issued by thc Jila Panchayat, Jashpur, it
was provided that the tenn of appointment may be extended on the basis of'

'^
f-100 ~
assessment and suitability, recommended by the Programme Officer. Some
ofthe appointment orders arising from Jashpur and other areas, providethat
the appointment ofRojgar Sahayak would be under the provisions ofRules,
2004. This condition is not provided in some of the appointment orders
issued by Janpad Panchayat Jagdalpur, Bilaspur etc. In some of the
appbintments, there was no provision for extension of term of the
appointment or applicability of the Rules, 2004. In case of Bilaspur and
Jagdalpur, there was no condition for extension or applicability of the
provisions of Rules, 2004 in the appointment order. In the appoinfmcnt
orders issued by Janpad Panchayats in districts Jashpur and Raigarh, there
was provision ofwriting confidential reports also.
Shri Rajeev Shrivastava, Shri F.S.Khare, Shri Santosh Bharat, Shri Dines
R.K.Tiwari, Dr. Rajesh Pandey, Shri A.N.Bhakta, Shri M.K.Sinha, Shri
A.N.Pandey and Shri Roop Naik, learned counsel appearing for tlie
respective petitioners would contend that the appointments oftbe petitioners
are governed by the provisions ofthe Niyam, 2004 wherein mle 1 1 providcs
for extension of period of appointment as per the necessity and on assessing
the suitability of the person appointed on contract. In cases of appointments
on the post ofAccountants and Technical Assistants, the appointment orders
provide for maintaining confidential reports and assessing the suitability of
the petitioners for further extension ofterm of appointment. Learned counsel
would further submit that issue of fresh advertisement for contractual
appointment is contrary to the provisions ofrule 11 ofthe Rules, 2004. The
direction ofthe State Govemment on 27.8.2007 to issue fresh advertisement
for appointment is bad in law. The contract appomtee cannot be substitutcd
by another contract appointee. The action of the resppndents is, thus,
violative of Article 14 and 21 of the Coristitution of India. It was further

101
contended that the appointment ofthe petitioners was in accordance with the
provisions of Rules, 2004 by inviting applications and holding proper
selection. The appointment ofthe petitioners cannot be held as illegal being
dehors the constitutional scheme of employment.
Shri Y.S.Thakur, leamed Deputy Advocate General appearing for the Stale,
Shri A.S.Kachhwaha, Shri Sumesh Bajaj, Shri Arun Sao, Shri leamed
counsel appearing for the State and respective Janpad Panchayats and Jila
Panchayats, Shri S.K.Beriwal and Shri Gajendra Sahu, leamed eounsel I'or
the Union of India, per contra, would submit that the appointment of the
Rojgar Sahayks, Technical Assistants and Accountants are not governed by
any rule. The administrative instructions issued time to time would be
applicable for appointments and other conditions of service. The petitioners
were appointed for the period ofone year. Their appointment came to an end
automatically after completion of a period of one year. The consideration of
the petitioners for extension of term of the appointment was not ignored as
10 marks weightage to the petitioners for having worked on contract basis on
the post of Rojgar Sahayaks, Accountants and Technical Assistants etc. was
;iven in the selection. It was funher contended that most ofthe petitioners
who are contract appointees, appointed earlier have already been selected and
they have been reappointed. Thus, their grievances do not survive.
Having heard learned counsel for the parties, perused the pleadings and
documents appended thereto, it is evident that the appointment of the
petitioners on the post of Rojgar Sahayaks, Technical Assistants,
Accountants and others, were under the National Rural Employment
Guarantee Scheme to assist and provide support as necessary for tlie
effective implementation ofthe scheme. The Parliament enacted the National
Rural Employment Guarantee Act, 2005 (for short "NREGA") to provide Ibr

sl
»-
^&G
102.
the enhancement of livelihood security ofthe households in rural areas oftlie
country by providing at least one hundred days of guaranteed wage
employment in every financial year to every household whose adult members
volunteer to do unskilled manual work and for matters connected therewith
or incidental thereto. It came into force on 7.9.2005. Section 4 ofNREGA
provides for Employment Guarantee Scheme for rural areas to unemployed
persons for not less than 100 days ofguaranteed employment in a financial
year to every household in the rural areas covered under the scheme. Section
13 ofNREGA provides for appointment ofPrincipal authorities forplanning
and implementation of scheme. Section 14 of NREGA provides that the
Chief Executive Officer of the District Panchayat or the Collector of the
Disfrict or any other District Officer- of the appropriate rank as the State
Govemment may decide, shall be designated as the District Programnie
Coordinator for the implementation of the schemes made under NREGA in
the district. The District Programme coordinator shall be responsible Ibr
implementation of the schemes in the district in accordance with the
provisions of NREGA and mles framed thereunder. The functions of the
District Programme Coordinator was also provided. Section 15 ofNREGA
provides for appointment of Programme Officer at intermediate level by
appointing a person not below the rank ofBlock Development Officer willii
such qualifications as may be determined by the State Government. It further
provides for funclions and responsibilities of the Programme Officer.
Responsibilities ofthe Gram Panchayats were also provided under section 16
ofNREGA. Section 18 provides for responsibility ofthe State Governmenl
in implementing the scheme wherein the State Government was also held
liable to make available to the District Programme Coordinator and the

^-
103
Programme Officer necessary staff and technical support as may be
necessary forthe effective implementation ofthe scheme.
8. Thereafter, the Central Government issued guidelines for implementation ol'
the schemes. In Clause 2.2. l(b) of the guidelines, it was provided that the
Rojgar Guarantee Sahayaks or Gram Rojgar Sahayak, by whatever naine it
may be called, may be appointed in order to implement provisions of
NREGA. Under section 4(1) of the NREGA. The State framed a scheme
constituting State Level Chhattisgarh State Employment Guarantee Council
and other agencies at different levels. It was made clear in the clause 2.1.10
ofthe State Scheme that 50% ofthe work shall be perfoniied by the Gram
Panchayat. One Rojgar Sahayak for one or two gram panchayats may be
appointed on contract basis by the Chief Executive Officer, Janpad
Panchayat on the basis of recommendation made by the Programme officer
and approved by the District Coordinator.
9. The Governor of Chhattisgarh, in exercise of its powers conferred by tlie
proviso to Article 309 ofthe Constitution oflndia, made Chhattisgarh Civil
Sewa (Samvida Niyukti) Niyam, 2004. The rules provides for contract
appointments. Rule 11 ofthe Rules, 2004 reads as under:
"11. Period ofAppointment-Normally contract appointment
shall be nerely for a year. The department may take decision
to extend the period of appointment as per the necessity and
on assessing the suitability of the person appointed on
contract. On expiry ofthe period ofthe Contract appointment,
automatically contract appointment shall stand terminated. "
10. Accordingly, a circular was issued on 21.03.2006 wherein it was providcd
that the term of appointment for one year may be extended on the basis ot'
recommendation made by the Programme Officer. Subsequently, one more
circular was issued on 19.12.2007 providing that appointment of Rojgar
Sahayaks in accordance with following guidelines, which reads as under:

11. It is therefore evident that the appointment of the petitioner was for a period
ofone year on contract basis. The advertisement dated 25.03.2006 (Annexure
P/l to W.P.(S) No. 7006/2007) inviting applications for appointment on
contract basis on the posts of Technical Assistant, Accountants, provides
inter alia, following conditions.

12. Condition No. 7 and 8 ofthe appointment order, mter alia, reads as under:

13. The above stated conditions were provided in cases ofTechnical Assistants
and Accountants only. There was no such condition in the advertisement
issued for appointment on the post of Rojgar Sahayaks. Only in some of the
appointment orders of the Rojgar Sahayaks, the condition for extension of
term of appointment on the basis of confidential report and assessment made
by the Programme Officers, was provided, not in all the appointments.
14. In view ofthe above stated statutory provisions and various circulars issucd
time to time, it is crystal clear that the petitioners were appointed on contract
basis for a period of one year. There was a provision for extension oftime of
the contract on the basis ofassessment oftheir performance as recommended
by programme officer. The terms and conditions of their appointments are
govemment by provisions of the Rule 11 of the Rules, 2004. The State
Govemment, by circular dated 27.8.2007 (Annexure P/6 to W.P.(S) No. 7069
of 2007) that all the appointments except Assistant Project Officer, Publicity
Assistants and Programme Officer shall be done at District level for a period
of one year on contract basis.It was made clear that all the appointments
would be made after completion of the term of the appointment of the
petitioners. Therefore, no appointment was terminated by order of the State
Government before completion of one year. The appointment was made
thereafter at District level as per circular dated 19.12.2007 (Annexure A-6 to
W.P.(S) No. 7006/2007), wherein weightage of 10 marks was given for

;.. t.
106
experience of working as Rojgar Sahayak, Accountants and Technical
Assistants.
15. Rule 11 ofRules 2004, provides for extension ofappointment on assessing of
the suitability of the person appointed on contract, but the said rules further
provides that on expiry oftUe period ofcontract, appointment automatically
shall stand terminated. The petitioners have no right to continue after their
appointment came to an end. The condition that they will be considered tor
further extension on the basis oftheir performance has been satisfied while
granting 10 marks weightage to all the Rojgar Sahayaks, Accountants and
Technical Assistants who have obtained experience for working of one
year/6 months on contract appointment. It cannot be held that the action of
the Govemment by inviting applications from all eligible candidates for
appointment on the post of Rojgai Sahayak, Accountants, Technical
Assistants and others at district level was contrary to provisions ofRule 11 of
the Rules 2004 and terms and conditions ofthe appointment, ifany.
16. Contention of learned counsel appearing for the petitioners that granting 10
percent weightage to all the Rojgar Sahayaks, Accountants, Technical
Assistants and others who had worked for one year/6 months on eontract
basis sacrifices efficiency in the performance. If a contract appointee has
worked better than other contract appointee, on assessment, the better
performer would be granted more marks and have better chances of success
than other contract appointee, appear to be attractive, but the contentions
deserve to be rejected on simple ground that a better performer may obtain
more marks under other heads than the experience. Granting of 10 marks
weightage to one and all is neither contradictory to the terms of the
conditions ofthe advertisement and appointment order, in the cases where-ever applicable, nor provisions of mle 11 ofthe Rules, 2004. No interference

^-
107
is warranted in the policy decision ofthe Govemment as that does not appear
to be arbitrary, unreasonable, discriminatory or violative ofArticle 1 4 and 21
of the Constitution of India. The contention of leamed counsel appearing for
the respondent authorities that the first appointment of the petitioners on
contract basis was not uniform as some of the districts provided some
conditions and some districts provided other conditions as is evident from the
facts stated hereinabove. Thus, the State Govemment took a policy decision
to have uniform system having reservation and roster system in accordance
with law, merits acceptance.
17. The contention that the conto-act appointee cannot be substituted by another
set ofcontract appointee isnot relevant to the facts ofthe case as there is no
substitution of the contract appointees but the contract appointees are
permitted to participate in the selection process and 10 marks has been given
as weightage to them.
18. The Supreme Court, in the matter of State of Haryana and others v. Piara
Singh and others relied on by Mr. Rajiv Shrivastava, was dealing with the
appointment of Class III and Class IV employees on adhoc basis without
reference to the Public Service Commission or the Subordinate Services
Selection Board without adhering to the employment exchange requirements.
Thus, the appointments made therein were dehors the constitutional scheme
of employment. Their Lordships, in that context held that the adhoc or
contract appointment not in accordance with the statutory rules and
regulations cannot be replaced by adhoc or temporary employees. They may
be replaced only by regularly selected employees. In the present case, the
petitioners were appointed in accordance with the mles through proper
selection and the second appointment pursuant to the advertisement under
(1992) 4SCC 118

^A
^-
108
challenge was also in accordance with law and the petitioners were entitled to
10 marks weightage for their experiences.
19. The case ofMarathwada Agricultural University and others v. Marathwada
Krishi Vidyapith, M.S.K.S. and others, cited by Mr. Shrivastava, is not
relevant to the facts ofthe case as the issue involved is not the granting of
regular pay scale to the daily rated workers at par with the permanent
labourers.
20. The Supreme Coun, in the matter of Secretary, State ofKamataka and others
v. Umadevi (3) and others , while dealing with legitimate expectation has
observed as under:
"47. When a person enters a temporary employment or gets engagement
as a contractual or casual worker and the engagement is not based on a
proper selection as recognised by the relevant rules or procedure, he is
aware ofthe consequences ofthe appointment being temporary, casual
pr contractual in nature._ Such a p'erson cannot invoke the theory of
legitimate expeetation for being" confirmed in the post when an
appointment fo the post could be made only by following a proper
procedure for selection and in cases concemed, iri consultation with the
Public Service Commission. Therefore, the theory of legitimate
expectation camiot be successfully advanced by temporary, contractual
or casual employees. It cannot also be held that the State has held out
any promise" while engaging these persons either to continue them
where they are or to make them permanent. The State carmot
constitutionally make such a promise. If is also obvious that the theory
cannot be invoked to seek a positive relief of being made permanent in
the post."
21. It is well settled that the administrative instructions can be issued to fill up
the gaps and to supplement statutory provisions. In the cases on hand, the
executive instructions issued time to time are not contrary to the National
Guarantee Scheme as well as Rules, 2004 which have been made specifically
applicable to the appointment of the petitioners. (See Dhananjay Malik and
others v. State ofUttaranchal and others , and Union oflndia v. K.P.Joseph
andothers ).

109
22. In view of the well settled principles of law and for the reasons stated
hereinabove, I am of the considered opinion that the issue of fresh
advertisement for appointment on the post of Rojgar Sahayak, Technical
Assistant, Accountant and others, in accordance with Rules, 2004, keeping in
view the reservation policy, roster system etc., does not suffer from any
irregularity, illegality, arbitrariness and discrimination. The petitioners who
have not participated in the selection process pursuant to the fresh
advertisement are entitled to be considered for extension of their term of
appoiutment in accordance with Rule 11 read with terms and conditions of
the advertisement and appointment orders, if applicable. In other cases,
where the petitioners have been granted 10 marks weightage on account of
their experience in the selection process and have failed to make in the
selection process are not entitled to consideration when they have already
been granted 10 marks as weightage for their experience as contract
appomtees.
23. It is well settled that a candidates who have appeared in the selection process
without demur cannot question the selection process after having failed in tlie
selection. {See Dhananjay Mallik (supra)}
24. For the reasons stated hereinabove, all the petitions are dismissed. Costs
easy.
25. The question of law involved in Review Petition No. 05/2008, is the saine
which has been considered in the above stated petitions. Thus, the review
petition also stands dismissed, accordingly.
